# Technical Reference: IronClaude issue-to-work pipeline (origin/master 61ac2c2e6)

## Task Overview

This task produces one Technical Reference document, `ISSUE-TO-WORK-PIPELINE-TECHNICAL-REFERENCE.md`, for the "issue-to-work pipeline" of the IronClaude repository as it exists on origin/master commit 61ac2c2e6. The pipeline consists of four cooperating pieces: (1) the issue watcher (`superclaude issue-watch once`, package `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/`), which polls GitHub read-only and launches at most one Orca agent per cycle; (2) the `/sc:issue --claim` flow (command, `sc-issue-protocol` skill, tier-pinned worker agents, `orca_worktree.py`), which performs every GitHub and ClickUp write for a claim and creates a worktree and agent at a `--pipeline` level; (3) the launch-level guard stack (L1 guard script, job identity, L2 pre-push hook, hooks registration, ccsession shim coupling, G2 rule 2); and (4) the coordinator contract text (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md`). The reference also carries a clearly labelled appendix "Removed on 2026-11-20" documenting the package `src/superclaude/cli/issue_pipeline/` and rule WC-17 as they stood just before PR 682 (commit 01e7a9e23) deleted them.

The reader of the reference is the repository owner, who needs an exact, cited picture of what is built and what is not, in order to decide how to unify two competing lines of work in a later design brainstorm. The reference therefore emphasizes triggers, state stores, approval and gate points, launch levels, guard layers, what runs live versus what is switched off (`enabled: false`), what each piece writes to GitHub, and every test file that pins behaviour. PR 686 and the rewrite design are out of scope; the reference says so in exactly one sentence in Section 1 and describes them nowhere else.

The work follows the tech-reference skill pipeline: six parallel code-research agents, a research completeness gate, a (skipped, rationale-recorded) web research phase, six synthesis agents with a synthesis gate, single-agent assembly, a seven-agent lens QA gate, a two-agent source-fidelity gate, and completion. QA intensity is `standard`. Every claim in the final document cites `path:line` against the 61ac2c2e6 snapshot, and every documentation-sourced claim carries a `[CODE-VERIFIED]`, `[CODE-CONTRADICTED]` or `[UNVERIFIED]` tag.

## Key Objectives

The following objectives MUST be achieved by this task:

1. **Six verified research files:** `research/01-...md` to `research/06-...md` in the task folder, each produced by reading the live snapshot source, each with a test-pin table, a "what runs live vs switched off" status line, a "writes to GitHub" list with the exact command, triggers, state stores and gates, and every doc-sourced claim tagged.
2. **Six template-aligned synthesis files** `synthesis/synth-01..06-*.md` that pass the standard-intensity synthesis gate.
3. **One assembled reference document** at `/home/coder/workspaces/IronClaude/.dev/brainstorms/20261120-pr686-unify/techref/ISSUE-TO-WORK-PIPELINE-TECHNICAL-REFERENCE.md`, in template section order, containing: (a) a live-vs-switched-off ledger (one row per piece: watcher, claim, L1, L2, identity, G2 rule 2, coordinator text, removed package; status words BUILT-ON / BUILT-OFF (enabled:false) / NOT BUILT / REMOVED, with evidence); (b) a "writes to GitHub" table per piece with exact commands; (c) a trigger table; (d) a test-pin matrix with one row per test file named in research-notes.md EXISTING_FILES (path, lines, what it pins); (e) a doc-vs-code contradiction table; (f) Appendix "Removed on 2026-11-20"; (g) exactly one sentence stating PR 686 / the rewrite design is out of scope and no other description of it.
4. **Defect candidates recorded, not hidden:** every in-scope capability that a design doc names as built or intended but that is unwired, switched off, retired or has no caller appears in Section 14 as a DEFECT CANDIDATE or NOT BUILT row with the declaring doc line and the code evidence; Section 14 states that no remediation tasks were created because this run may not write outside its task folder and the output file.
5. **All four QA gates passed** (research completeness, synthesis quality, final-document lens QA, source fidelity) within the 2-fix-cycle limit, or the run HALTED and escalated to the agent named `team-lead` with the open blocking findings.

### Key Constraints
- QA intensity `standard` (per I22 and BUILD-REQUEST): Gate 1 = 3 agents; Gate 2 = 3 lenses x 2 partitions; Gate 3 = 7 agents; Gate 4 = 2 fidelity agents; verification rounds = 2 agents; max 2 fix cycles per gate, then HALT and escalate by SendMessage to the agent named `team-lead` with the open blocking findings (minor findings stay as notes). Standard intensity supersedes the skill's 5-agent floor, as BUILD-REQUEST states.
- Read-only source discipline: source only from `/home/coder/.cache/ic-snap-20261120/master`; removed code only via `git -C /home/coder/workspaces/IronClaude show 01e7a9e23^:<path>`; writes only inside the task folder and the single output file; no staging or committing; read-only `gh` with `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`; UV only; no web research; memory notes and chat claims are not sources.
- Granularity and parallelism: one checklist item per agent. Consecutive items that spawn independent report-only or research agents within the same phase MAY be spawned in parallel in one message (F2a exception) but each item is marked individually. The executor never pauses for scope or cost reasons.
- **Report-validity rule (applies to every gate):** a QA or analyst report COUNTS toward a gate only if (a) the file exists, (b) it carries `Status: Complete` (or the equivalent closing marker its prompt requires, for example `## QA Complete`), and (c) it has a line-anchored verdict line that begins the line with `VERDICT: PASS`, `VERDICT: FAIL`, `## Verdict: PASS|FAIL` or `## Overall Verdict: PASS|FAIL` (the forms the prompts require; check with `grep -E '^(## )?(Overall )?(Verdict|VERDICT): (PASS|FAIL)'`). Required valid review-1 report counts: Gate 1 = 3, Gate 2 = 6, Gate 3 = 7, Gate 4 = 2. If an expected report is missing or invalid, re-spawn that single agent ONCE with the same prompt; if it is still missing or invalid, record a synthetic BLOCKING finding (severity HIGH, source `synthetic-dnsp`, affected range = that agent's assigned lens and files, recommendation `Manual review required: partition agent failed twice`) in the gate's consolidated findings file, so it is counted in `F_1` and the gate cannot pass silently. A synthetic finding cannot be fixed by an agent: it gets no gap-fill item and no fix edit, it stays open in every verification and decision (counted in `F_2` and `F_3`), and only manual review by the owner via `team-lead` clears it. The same validity test applies to the two verification reports of each round, which the decision items read. Only QA-gate agent items carry this re-spawn-and-synthesize rule; the "log the blocker and mark complete" escape of non-QA items is unchanged.

  CRITICAL - Documentation Staleness Protocol:
  Documentation describes intent or historical state. Code describes CURRENT state. These frequently diverge. When you encounter documentation (design docs under docs/designs/, runbooks, command or skill prose, code comments) that describes an architecture, pipeline, command, component, flag, constant or workflow, you MUST cross-validate EVERY structural claim against actual code before reporting it as current:

  (2) Pipelines and call chains described in docs: trace at least the first and last hop in source; if any hop is missing, the chain is STALE.
  (3) File paths mentioned in docs: spot-check that each exists; if renamed, moved or removed mark [CODE-CONTRADICTED] with the actual path.
  (4) CLI verbs, flags, constants, numeric values, exit codes, reason words and state names in docs: Grep the code and compare; a mismatch is [CODE-CONTRADICTED] with the code value.
  For EVERY doc-sourced claim mark exactly one tag: [CODE-VERIFIED] (confirmed by reading source at path:line), [CODE-CONTRADICTED] (code shows something different; state what), [UNVERIFIED] (no corresponding code found; may be stale, planned or elsewhere).
